Every Pokédex entry from the first number to the last, both included, is marked seen and caught when you open the Pokédex from the Start menu. For version 1.0.26.

  • Tick it, open POKéDEX from the Start menu, then untick it: what it marked is saved with your game like anything you catch.
  • The numbers are the National Pokédex numbers the game shows (0001 Bulbasaur to 1025 Pecharunt).
  • It only adds: nothing already seen or caught is taken away, and nothing outside the range changes.
  • The Pokédex's own SEEN and OWN counts include them. It marks entries; it doesn't give you the Pokémon.
  • Made for 1.0.26 only.

Every species

Every Pokémon in the game with its ID, types and abilities: 1575 entries, 555 of them forms. Type lists the species with that type, alone or with another; pick a second type for that pair, or No second type for the ones with only the first. Copy code copies the Pick the species code for that species; Moves shows what it learns (hover a move for a summary, click it to open it in Moves & TMs). Click or tap a Pokémon's name for its card: sprite (Shiny and Back under it show the shiny colours and the back), base stats, catch rate, gender ratio, how it evolves and where to find it (click a Pokémon in its evolution line to switch the card to it). Hover or tap an ability on the card for what it does; Where to find searches the Encounter map for it. The card stays open while you scroll, until you close it (×, Escape) or click elsewhere; drag it by its top bar (or Move) to put it anywhere, or Pin it where it is. Searching for an ability's exact name lists the species that have it.

Copy code changes

A form the game gives a regional or Mega name is listed with it (Alolan, Mega, Gigantamax…); the game names its other forms only by their species, so those are listed as Form 2, Form 3… in the order of its form tables. The Dex column is the national Pokédex number.

Under the hoodFill the Pokédex (1.0.26), the raw species template, where the data, sprites and evolutions come from

Fill the Pokédex

Fill the Pokédex

The Pokédex keeps one bit per National Pokédex number (1–1,025; bit n − 1): seen at SaveBlock1 + 0x2BD4 and caught at SaveBlock1 + 0x2C55, 129 bytes each (found by catching a Kecleon, #352, and comparing the save before and after). The game's GetSetPokedexFlag (0x081B6E58: the number, then 0 or 1 to read seen or caught, 2 or 3 to set them) is the only code that writes them, and the Pokédex's counts (0x081B6EC8) read them. The code points the Start menu's POKéDEX entry (0x08DA43BC) at a routine at 0x09FFB000 (asm/dex_fill.s). Each time it runs it calls GetSetPokedexFlag with 2 and with 3 for every number from the first to the last, then goes on to the game's own callback (0x0821B39C), which opens the Pokédex once the screen has faded. The game calls it on every frame of that fade, and setting a bit that's already set changes nothing. The range is the two words after the routine; the routine takes a first number below 1 as 1 and a last above 1,025 as 1,025, so it can't write past the two arrays.

The routine (Thumb assembly, asm/dex_fill.s)
@ Generation 3 Evolved 1.0.26: "Fill the Pokedex" (seen and caught for a range of National Pokedex numbers). Assembled
@ at DEX_BASE (codes.py, 0x09FFB000, in the ROM's free space); the code writes START and END (the range's first and
@ last numbers, inclusive) into the two words after it. tests.py asm checks that the bytes codes.py stores still match.
@
@ The Pokedex keeps one bit per National Pokedex number (1-1025, bit n-1): seen at SaveBlock1 + 0x2BD4, caught at
@ SaveBlock1 + 0x2C55, 129 bytes each. The game's GetSetPokedexFlag (0x081B6E58: number, case) reads (0 seen, 1 caught)
@ and sets (2 seen, 3 caught) them; everything that counts the Pokedex (GetNationalPokedexCount 0x081B6EC8 and so on)
@ reads the same bits. The code points the Start menu's POKeDEX entry (sStartMenuItems[0].func, 0x08DA43BC) here: each
@ call sets seen and caught for every number from START to END through GetSetPokedexFlag, then goes on to the game's
@ StartMenuPokedexCallback (0x0821B39C), which opens the Pokedex once the screen has faded. The game calls it every
@ frame of the fade; setting a bit that is already set changes nothing. START below 1 is taken as 1 and END above 1025
@ as 1025, so nothing outside the two arrays is ever written.

DexFill:
    push {r4, r5, lr}
    ldr r4, first
    ldr r5, last
    cmp r4, #0
    bne 1f
    movs r4, #1
1:
    ldr r3, =COUNT
    cmp r5, r3
    bls loop
    movs r5, r3
loop:
    cmp r4, r5
    bhi done
    movs r0, r4
    movs r1, #2                @ FLAG_SET_SEEN
    ldr r3, =GetSetPokedexFlag
    bl via
    movs r0, r4
    movs r1, #3                @ FLAG_SET_CAUGHT
    ldr r3, =GetSetPokedexFlag
    bl via
    adds r4, #1
    b loop
done:
    pop {r4, r5}
    pop {r3}
    mov lr, r3
    ldr r3, =StartMenuPokedexCallback
    bx r3
via:
    bx r3
    .ltorg
    .balign 4
first:                         @ START: written by the code
    .word 0xFFFFFFFF
last:                          @ END: written by the code
    .word 0xFFFFFFFF
Raw form (1–150)

The range is the 2nd-to-last pair of line pairs before the hook: the first number (0x0001) and the last (0x0096 = 150), each with 0x0000 after it.

00000000 18FFD800 0000B530 00000000 00000000 18FFD801 00004C11 00000000 00000000 18FFD802 00004D11 00000000 00000000 18FFD803 00002C00 00000000 00000000 18FFD804 0000D100 00000000 00000000 18FFD805 00002401 00000000 00000000 18FFD806 00004B0B 00000000 00000000 18FFD807 0000429D 00000000 00000000 18FFD808 0000D900 00000000 00000000 18FFD809 0000001D 00000000 00000000 18FFD80A 000042AC 00000000 00000000 18FFD80B 0000D80B 00000000 00000000 18FFD80C 00000020 00000000 00000000 18FFD80D 00002102 00000000 00000000 18FFD80E 00004B08 00000000 00000000 18FFD80F 0000F000 00000000 00000000 18FFD810 0000F80C 00000000 00000000 18FFD811 00000020 00000000 00000000 18FFD812 00002103 00000000 00000000 18FFD813 00004B06 00000000 00000000 18FFD814 0000F000 00000000 00000000 18FFD815 0000F807 00000000 00000000 18FFD816 00003401 00000000 00000000 18FFD817 0000E7F1 00000000 00000000 18FFD818 0000BC30 00000000 00000000 18FFD819 0000BC08 00000000 00000000 18FFD81A 0000469E 00000000 00000000 18FFD81B 00004B03 00000000 00000000 18FFD81C 00004718 00000000 00000000 18FFD81D 00004718 00000000 00000000 18FFD81E 00000401 00000000 00000000 18FFD81F 00000000 00000000 00000000 18FFD820 00006E59 00000000 00000000 18FFD821 0000081B 00000000 00000000 18FFD822 0000B39D 00000000 00000000 18FFD823 00000821 00000000 00000000 18FFD824 00000001 00000000 00000000 18FFD825 00000000 00000000 00000000 18FFD826 00000096 00000000 00000000 18FFD827 00000000 00000000 00000000 186D21DE 0000B001 00000000 00000000 186D21DF 000009FF 00000000

Another run ticks it with every other code on the Your Pokémon tab.

Raw template

For the version picked above: the code's own lines, which come after the 296 lines every species, nature, gender and ability code starts with (Catching tab, Under the hood). Put a species' Hex ID in place of SSSS (both places) (all three places), and add the whole code as Autodetect.

Where the data comes from

The look-ups on this page are read from 1.0.26, the latest, and the tests below ran on it (the same tests passed on 1.0.20 and 1.0.24). Where the older versions differ: 1.0.25 gave 34 Pokémon that evolve by friendship a second way, by reaching a level (e.g. Golbat at 40), and added moves to 5 learnsets: 1.0.24 and 1.0.20 have neither. The addresses here are 1.0.20's, where this guide was first worked out; each is at most 0x780 bytes further on in 1.0.26.

0x087CA828
gSpeciesInfo (pointed at by the game's ROM header, +0xBC), 0x108 bytes per species: base stats +0 to +5 (HP, Attack, Defense, Speed, Sp. Atk, Sp. Def), the two types at +6 and +7 (the same one twice for a single type), catch rate +8, gender ratio +0x12 (0 male only, 254 female only, 255 genderless), the three abilities at +0x18, the name at +0x2C, the national Pokédex number at +0x3C, flags at +0x8C (legendary, Mythical, Ultra Beast, Paradox, and the form kind: Mega, Primal, Alolan…), the learnsets at +0x94, +0x98 and +0x9C, the evolutions at +0xA0 and the form table at +0xA4.
Forms
555 entries share their name with another (a regional form, a Mega, a Gigantamax…). They're species of their own here, with their own stats, types, abilities and codes. A card lists the species' Forms under its evolution family (the 135 battle forms, Mega, Primal, Ultra Burst and Gigantamax, marked Battle only), each a button that switches the card to it.
Only by evolution
367 species are met nowhere themselves (no wild table, gift, Egg, one-off battle or roaming slot) but their line starts with one that is: the card says Only by evolution and gives the steps from that one.
Sprites
The front picture's first frame (gSpeciesInfo +0x58) and the back picture (+0x5C) in the species' own colours (+0x60; the shiny ones +0x64). The pictures are packed in the expansion's own format, which the GBA itself can't unpack, so each was unpacked by the game's own routine (DecompressDataWithHeaderWram, 0x08114EB0) in mGBA. Entry 1172 has no picture.
Gender
The card's split is the game's own rule: female when the personality's lowest byte is below the species' ratio.
Evolutions
gSpeciesInfo +0xA0: a list per species of {method, parameter, species, conditions} (536 species evolve). The card's wording follows the method (Level N, Use X, Level N (in a battle), Spin around) and its conditions (a time of day, friendship, a held item, a move it knows, a place, the weather, a Pokémon in the party, the Attack/Defense split…). This hack added a condition of its own, with N coins in the Coin Case (checked with GetCoins in 0x081C9ED8).

The species card, tested

The base stats were checked against the game's own stat calculation (every species given with givemon at two levels; 47 forms that need a held item, such as Arceus's and Silvally's types and Origin Dialga, are given as their base form without it and were checked as that), the sprites in a battle's video memory, and the evolutions by level and by item with the game's own evolution check (990 cases, 495 of which evolve).

Wild encounters by location

Search by place, by Pokémon or by region, or pick a marker on the game's region maps (Kanto, Hoenn and the three Sevii Islands maps, side by side). Chances are per encounter. Click or tap a Pokémon's name for its card: sprite (shiny and back too), abilities, base stats and evolutions; its Where to find searches here. It stays open until you close it or click elsewhere; drag it by its top bar (or Pin it) and it stays on screen while you search other places and Pokémon.

One area. 6Several areas share that spot, and the number says how many. Click a marker or a place name to open it, and again to close it; on a numbered marker each click opens the next area. Hover a marker for its areas' names. On a narrow screen the map scrolls sideways.

  • Altering Cave has nine sets of Pokémon; the game picks one by a variable in your save (the first set until something changes it), and its list shows them all together.
  • Bell Tower, Dragonspiral Tower, Lunar Isle, Secret Den, Sendoff Spring, Slumbering Weald and Turnback Cave aren't on any of the region maps, so they're only in the list of places.
  • Gifts, Eggs and one-off battles (133, read from the game's map scripts) are under each place's Also here, and searching a Pokémon's name finds them under Other ways. 15 places have only those. Each line says how to start it: where to go, what to talk to, check or step on, and what has to happen first (a badge, an item, a puzzle, another legendary caught), and whether you get a second chance. A Pokémon's card says the same for one that isn't in the wild, and how to get the rest (evolving, a starter, a battle form).
  • Search a region (Hoenn, Kanto, Sevii Islands 1–3, Sevii Islands 4–5 and Sevii Islands 6–7) to list every gift, Egg, one-off battle and roaming Pokémon of the places on that region's map, by place ("Sevii" gives all three Sevii Islands maps). 11 places with them aren't on any region map, and 20 roaming Pokémon keep to no one place: the list names them under it.
  • Roaming Pokémon. 19 keep to one or a few places, listed there (Also here); 20 more (Raikou, Entei, Suicune, Tornadus, Thundurus, Enamorus, Tapu Koko, Tapu Lele, Tapu Bulu, Tapu Fini, Cobalion, Terrakion, Virizion, Wo-Chien, Chien-Pao, Ting-Lu, Chi-Yu, Okidogi, Munkidori and Fezandipiti) roam more widely, and their cards say so. Each one's line and card say what sets it loose.
Under the hoodWhere the tables come from, how the game picks one, and how they were checked

Where the data comes from

The look-ups on this page are read from 1.0.26, the latest, and the tests below ran on it (the same tests passed on 1.0.20 and 1.0.24). Where the older versions differ: 1.0.24 and 1.0.20 fish up Magikarp, not Arrokuda, with the Old Rod in Pallet Town, and Shaymin roams around Five Isle Meadow at Lv60, not Lv30. The addresses here are 1.0.20's, where this guide was first worked out; each is at most 0x780 bytes further on in 1.0.26.

0x08F4D5E8
The wild Pokémon headers: 383 of them for 375 maps, each with a grass/cave, surfing, Rock Smash and fishing table (a rate, then 12, 5, 5 and 10 slots with a level range and a species). The expansion keeps room for four times of day; this game fills only the first, and its encounter code always asks for that one.
0x08245244
GetCurrentMapWildMonHeaderId: the header for the map you're on. Altering Cave (map 24.104) has nine, and the game picks one by var 0x4024.
Slot odds
Grass and caves 20, 20, 10, 10, 10, 10, 5, 5, 4, 4, 1, 1 %; surfing and Rock Smash 60, 30, 5, 4, 1 %; Old Rod 70, 30 %, Good Rod 60, 20, 20 %, Super Rod 40, 40, 15, 4, 1 %. A level is picked between the slot's two.
Places
Each map's name and where it sits on a region map come from its map section (gMapGroups 0x090C5690, map header +0x14; names and squares in gRegionMapEntries, 0x08D94F00). Which of the five region maps a section is on was asked of the game itself (GetRegionMapType, 0x081FC124). Maps under one name with the same tables are one place (the Map line lists them); places with the same name and different tables are numbered Area 1, Area 2… (144 areas).
Regions
The region search goes by the same answer: the region map the game shows for a place's map section (GetRegionMapType, 0x081FC124, asked in mGBA for every section). Past section 0xC0 the game answers Hoenn for all of them, so those count only where the section is drawn on the Hoenn map (Aqua Hideout, Mirage Tower, Artisan Cave…); the hack's own places that aren't drawn (Spear Pillar, Crown Shrine…) are in no region.
Map
The game's five region map pictures (gRegionMapInfos, 0x08D90638), unpacked by the game's own routine in mGBA and put side by side. A marker sits on the middle square of a place's area.
Other ways
extract_obtain.py follows every map's scripts (its people, signs, step triggers and on-arrival scripts, through calls, gotos and trainer battles' follow-up scripts, with the expansion's command lengths) and lists each gift (givemon, the expansion's callnative ScrCmd_createmon), giveegg, setwildbattle and legendary made by special 0x201, under the map's section name. A gift whose species comes from a choice is listed once for each: the starters (the three species the lab's script sets), the fossils (the game's fossil table, 0x08601B64) and the Paradox Pokémon (the game's own specials 0xC4 and 0xC5, run in mGBA for every species to see which ones they turn into a Paradox Pokémon).
How each starts
Read from each one's map scripts and the game code they call (1.0.26's addresses): the object or tile that runs the script, the flags, variables and items it checks on the way (a badge's flag is set by that Gym Leader's win script; the Hall of Fame's by its own code), the map script that shows or hides it (a Pokémon whose "knocked out or ran" flag is set isn't shown again), the Regi tombs' wall checks (each field move's check of the tile you stand on and which way you face, and Regice's lap of 36 tiles), the Secret Dens (the indent's script uses Secret Power from the Bag's HM09), the old woman's Obol table (0x0860211C: Obol, map, tile) and the roamer specials 309-334, each the game's own call to the roamer setup for one or more slots.
Roaming
The roamer setup function (0x081FE6A0: slot, species, level) is called from the game's code for 20 of them; the other 19 come from a table at 0x08D977D8 (species, slot, level, and the maps it moves between).
Not found
208 entries (mostly forms whose species is found, such as Pikachu's and Unown's other forms, and the Totem forms) are in no wild table, gift, Egg, one-off battle or roaming slot, and nothing evolves into them. They may come from something this search doesn't read (an item that changes a form, a battle), or not be obtainable.

Tested in the game

Each test starts from the test save on Route 1 and calls the game's own functions through a stub, so every roll is the game's: the header lookup for every map, and 2,000 rolls of TryGenerateWildMon or the fishing roll per table, each Pokémon's share within 4.5 standard deviations of the page's and its levels in range.

How each one starts (1.0.26): 126 runs

From the test save with no codes: the player is put beside the trigger, faces it and presses A (or steps onto the spot), then A through the text. With the line's requirements set it must start that battle or give that Pokémon; with each one taken away, nothing. A battle Pokémon knocked out with one move must be gone when the map is entered again. Steps the save can't play through are run where the game decides them: the fossils handed over and the room left and entered again, the next-day scientists with their day counter moved on, the Regi walls' field-move checks at the right tile and facing (and not one tile off), Regice's lap walked for real.

Please read before using any code. Every Action Replay code on this site was worked out by Claude (an AI model), which reverse-engineered the game's .gba ROM file and then ran each code in the target emulator named on the guide. This website was vibe-coded with Claude as well.

I maintain this site, and I have not personally tested every code documented here. I've only tried a few, the ones I needed in my own playthroughs.

Always back up your save file before using any code.

Every code targets one exact ROM build: pick your version (1.0.20, 1.0.24 or 1.0.26) at the top of the page, and check the ROM's CRC32 (Start here tab) against your file before using it. No ROMs are hosted here.
